The thirteen native pages above total **1,295 retrieved occurrences**, not 1,295 independent works or full readings. Every title was inspected. The following supplied abstracts/index texts were also consumed: C01 p1 positions **1, 2, 10, 11, 16, 19, 20, 26, 29, 37, 40, 42**; C01 p2 **12, 13, 20, 23, 25, 36, 43, 62, 67, 72, 77, 99**; C03 **5, 9, 10, 18, 19, 32, 54, 57, 63, 74, 95, 97**; C04 **2, 13, 19, 32, 46, 59, 67, 75, 86, 90, 97**; C05 **4, 6, 46, 57, 90, 98**; C06 **2, 3, 5, 9, 12, 20, 23, 27, 30, 49, 55, 61, 85, 99**; C07 **2, 6, 13, 21, 23, 36, 41, 50, 68, 74, 87, 90, 100**. C02 now has a [recorded selective screen](nu-background-types-search-2026-09-30.md): 41 passages on page 1 and 42 on page 2, including truncated/single-sentence records; 116 abstracts remain unexamined. This does not exhaust either query or theme.

C01 p1 position 99 has a mismatched anaesthesia-editorial DOI and functional-programming text; no claim is credited to that identity. C03 includes durable nonvolatile-memory studies whose meaning of persistence differs from versioned functional structures, and a title collision between introductory material and Okasaki's book. C04 surfaces contrary/null interpretations of high coupling and metric-based maintainability. The two Yamashita 2013 records (`10.1007/s10664-013-9250-3`, `10.1016/j.infsof.2013.08.002`) may share their six-developer/four-system dataset; the Oliva 2015 paper and later thesis may also overlap. Those are lineage checks, not independent replications. Arisholm's actual-change weighting (`10.1016/j.infsof.2006.01.002`) must not be imported as a decision-time variable. These are primary-reading leads, not borrowed effect estimates.

## Audit status

The completed S71 reading and its direct citation/type-authoring follow-up set were submitted once as `nu_background_s71_20260930`. Scite accepted **two credited source identities and eight not-credited identities, zero skipped**. The two credited identities are one work and its author appendix. Six relevant leads remain deferred (mapped to `excluded/out_of_scope` because the tool has no deferred status), one circuit paper is off-topic and one proceedings record is not an independent study. S73's `full_text` stage explicitly means only its opening pages were read.

The inspected `citation_report` showed ten considered identities, no missing reasons, no retrieval-link warning and no truncation; answer-scoped retrieved count was null. This audit covers that completed segment, not all 897 native Consensus occurrences or unchanged historical decisions. The later Casanova and X01 audits below cover separate completed sets; native-query screening and new primary reading remain in progress. The tool's screening terminology does not certify systematic-review completeness.


The completed Casanova segment was reported once as `nu_background_casanova_20260930`: **37 identities, three credited, 34 not credited, zero skipped**. The credited sources are S69, S70 and the complete 70-byte networking wiki (only for its artifact location). The other identities comprise 26 off-topic name matches, seven relevant family/neighbor leads deferred for primary or lineage examination, and the access-limited networking ZIP. The report contains 33 title/abstract decisions, three full-text decisions and one unstaged inaccessible artifact; no missing reasons, linkage warning or truncation, and an answer-scoped retrieved count of null. A deferred lead is not rejected evidence.

X01 was reported once as `nu_background_x01_20260930`: **20 identities, 19 credited as discovery metadata/abstracts, one off-topic exclusion, zero skipped**, all at title/abstract stage. The inspected report has no missing reasons, linkage warning or truncation; retrieved count is null. Crediting a discovery lead does not credit a method, result or full reading. The complete report preserves score inputs, missing citation counts, unexamined continuations and all four connector errors.

## Primary repository follow-up and promoted readings

W01 used four web queries: `"Typed Design Patterns for the Functional Era" pdf`; `"Video Game Bad Smells" pdf Nardone`; `"entity component system" "performance" evaluation thesis`; and `"Maintainability Myth Causes Performance Problems" pdf`. This follows C06/C07's poor ECS-specific recall. Primary author/repository records surfaced S74–S77 below, Harris's [ECS implementation thesis](https://digitalcommons.calpoly.edu/theses/2389/), Vagedes's [Rust architecture thesis](https://scholar.afit.edu/etd/3191/), a [Czech ECS case study](https://dspace.cuni.cz/handle/20.500.11956/192941), [MassEntity simulation thesis](https://webthesis.biblio.polito.it/39951/), and the preprints [ECS essence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14919), [ECS concurrency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15264) and [world crafting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.16952). These are unresolved leads, not measured effects or independent replications. The controlled [2D scalability preprint](https://papers.ssrn.com/sol3/papers.cfm?abstract_id=6854635) leaves pooled OOP as future work in its abstract; its headline comparison needs that control checked. The [flocking thesis](https://repository.upi.edu/119643/) has restricted chapters 2/4 and appendix, and its abstract distinguishes scripting speed from complete animated performance. No social-forum or aggregator technical claims are adopted.

W02 checked exact titles for S76's institution, S77's degree, Gibson/Senn's system-structure study and the maintainability-myth paper. The first two identities are confirmed at the primary repositories. [DBLP's APSEC record](https://dblp.org/rec/conf/apsec/HagganderBBL99) gives a different published title, *Maintainability Myth Causes Performance Problems in SMP Application*; a [CiteSeer author PDF](https://citeseerx.ist.psu.edu/document?doi=c05bb86c452e7bf4d47046dfaf0040a850860273&repid=rep1&type=pdf) uses *Parallel Applications*. Publication lineage and full methods remain to verify. Gibson/Senn's `10.1145/62065.62073` is a priority primary acquisition because it measures maintenance tasks, while subjective and static metrics may diverge. These searches also surfaced a [2025 Eurographics paper](https://diglib.eg.org/bitstreams/766b72a4-70ae-4e8e-935b-949d589ed962/download) citing both S76 and S77; its identity/body remains to inspect. A citing paper is not automatically independent evidence.

The user explicitly requested Zotero records for intended readings. S72–S77 were checked across the local library by exact DOI where available, normalized title and canonical URL before creation. Native API readback verifies **six new parents and five uploaded PDFs** in `PKLXQNEE`, with topical memberships and notes describing the live claim and actual reading state. Current holdings are **96 parents = 93 literature + three public-source records, 103 PDFs, seven subcollections**. ## S74 completion and further foundation records

[S74's complete reading](full-reading/S74-typed-design-patterns.md) covers all nine author-PDF pages, seventeen listings, the single figure and all 24 bibliography entries. Pages 2–8 were visually inspected. Prior typed mechanisms and complexity tradeoffs are established; the public-empty-witness and nested-vector/downcast inconsistencies are printed-example deductions, not executed failures. No comparative maintenance effect is measured. Zotero note `KC8JKIPU` now carries the full reading, and parent `5N995MVS` is tagged complete. This supersedes its acquired-only state in the earlier import table.

SC07 queried the two exact maintainability-myth titles with `limit=20`, offset 0, returning **one** APSEC record (`10.1109/apsec.1999.809644`). SC08 queried S74's six exact bibliography DOIs listed in its note with `limit=20`, offset 0, returning **six** records. All returned metadata, supplied short/missing abstracts and available citation snippets were inspected. Exact-identity matches do not exhaust either mechanism family.

W03 used `"Maintainability Myth" filetype:pdf Bosch` and `"System structure and software maintenance performance" Gibson Senn filetype:pdf`, followed by `site:janbosch.com "Maintainability Myth"` and `"Maintainability Myth" "Software Engineering and Applications" 1999`. The primary [ARTES project publication list](https://www.artes.uu.se/project/publications/list.shtml) separately lists SEA October 1999 and APSEC December 1999 papers. Consequently S79's seven-page [indexed author paper](https://www.janbosch.com/Articles/SEA99haggander.pdf) does **not** receive the four-page APSEC DOI. A full primary comparison is still required before interpreting their study overlap. The direct author URL redirects to a 404, despite the web index exposing PDF text; CiteSeer redirects to a Wayback 404. These are acquisition failures, not absence of a readable indexed body. S78's ACM PDF returns 403. S76's encoded-URL and university-host PDF alternatives also return 403. No global access impossibility is inferred.

S78–S80 are recorded in Zotero after identity checks, adding **three parents and no PDFs**: current holdings **99 parents = 96 literature + three public records, 103 PDFs**. None of these three has full-reading credit. The newly selected records make the intended readings and acquisition gaps visible without waiting for another prompt.

## S72 completed primary reading

[S72](full-reading/S72-functional-programmer-authoring.md) is now fully read: thirty pages, eight figures, four tables, all tasks/hypotheses and bibliography. Renders of pp. 4, 6, 8 and 16–21 cover every figure/table. Printed counts and nine Wilson intervals were reconstructed; participant videos, coding and experiments were not repeated. The fixed-commit livestream repository contains only a README with fifteen identities, five labeled expired, rather than quantitative raw data. Earlier CHI data are explicitly identified by the main paper as part of the same grounded-theory lineage; exact participant overlap remains unverified.

This source directly motivates the wildcard/evolution tradeoff and deliberate compiler feedback use. Its one-task Haskell contrast is pattern matching/recursion versus predefined combinators, six participants each: median time 3.7/10.3 minutes (reported p=.004), weighted NASA-TLX 34.8/60.2 (p=.083, not significant at .05). Conditional refactoring compilation (6/8 among those who compiled), pooled-name signal counts and author-observed correctness must not become population prevalence, independent outcomes or behavioral-oracle validation. It does not establish an exhaustive-versus-fallback agent effect.

Three cohorts retained 20, 30 and 21 participant slots for one PL/I simulator and two changes. Unique-person overlap is unverified; this is not three applications. An initial parameter-interface timing advantage does not recur. Think time excludes editing, the visible tests govern stopping, and attrition/time limits remain. The experiment manipulates one interface dimension; it does not refute every information-hiding mechanism. Its metric derivation can rank the same designs differently from other coupling metrics, illustrating why a structural score is not itself an observed maintenance benefit.

[S89](full-reading/S89-information-hiding-decomposition.md) is fully read: six pages of the 1972 journal, API examples, footnotes and eleven references, with every page rendered. It treats modules as assignments of responsibility for changeable decisions, and distinguishes that source organization from runtime data/algorithm layout. Selected anticipated changes motivate locality; unnecessary interface obligations can still constrain change. Procedure overhead and representation-mapping tools are explicit costs. The classroom/compiler accounts provide no quantitative maintenance comparison. Both the prior mechanisms and these limits matter to Nu's innovation claims.

Metadata and supplied short/missing abstracts were inspected. The 1972/1983 specification editions, 1971-cited/2012-indexed report, and 1976/1978 family editions require primary comparison. Contracts, extension and family theory are consequential open continuations, not extra full readings.

## S84 complete, inheritance lineage and four new foundation attachments

[S84](full-reading/S84-task-dependent-inheritance.md) is fully read: twelve publisher pages, five tables, both figures, scoring rules and eleven references. Five new renders cover all tables/figures; the first page was checked at acquisition. Reconstructing the 53 retained participants' printed times/rounded scores recovers the main summaries and tests. The small experiment-2 productivity p-value difference (.00339 reconstructed versus .004 printed) retains possible rounding/software conventions. No original edits, unrounded grading, source equivalence or experiment were reproduced.

Three different student cohorts perform three selected changes to the same loan-report program pair. Deterministic course-rank balancing is not described randomization. Inheritance favors the common-heading task; flat correctness favors changing one leaf's inherited behavior. The third, five-per-group comparison has no significant difference, but no equivalence test: its pooled-model time-difference interval is approximately −9.05 to +9.45 minutes. The graded score discounts compilation failures, and tool/feedback availability is not fully specified. These findings support concrete task/design tradeoffs with explicit boundaries, not a general architecture ranking. Complete publication coverage is **76 = thirteen P + three A + sixty S**.

## S91/S92 complete: measured structure, effort and new contrary-evidence PDFs

[S91](full-reading/S91-structured-design-quality.md) is fully read: all eight publisher pages, 21 metric definitions, four tables, the figure and ten references. Seven page images verify the identity, definitions and all quantitative displays. Its 73 design units come from one industrial project and seven designers; two designers contribute 56 units. Frozen pre-coding charts are related to modification counts during integration/system testing, with causes left unclassified. The reported 50–60% variance explanation comes from same-data stepwise models, not held-out prediction, prevented failures or reduced maintenance effort. Visually verified variable/category discrepancies and chart-detail limitations remain in the reading. This is useful association evidence, alongside S82's different intervention and endpoint.

SC22 requested the exact Yin/Winchester design-measures title, Lattanzi methodology title and Daly/Mnichowicz stored-program switching title, `limit:20`, offset 0. **Four records** returned: `10.1145/953579.811099`, `10.1145/1007775.811099`, `10.1145/800283.811099` and `10.1109/CMPSAC.1979.762457`. The first three are edition/venue identities for Yin/Winchester, not three independent experiments. Lattanzi's conference/tutorial correspondence and Yin's indexed author-name discrepancy remain unresolved; Daly was unmatched. All supplied metadata and short/missing abstracts were inspected. W17 used the exact Yin/Winchester and Daly/Mnichowicz title queries; eighteen web identities were screened. Troy's thesis and earlier measurement bodies remain open.

[S92](full-reading/S92-information-hiding-maintenance.md) is fully read: all seven publisher pages, protocol and structural-count prose, and 28 references. Six page images verify the identity and consequential result/summary passages. Fourteen of 22 students complete two Ada changes, with eight missing participants and program version assigned by campus attendance. Most good boundaries are retained; some exposed code gains access routines, while a good boundary also deteriorates. The paper reports no correlation with its effort measures but supplies no effects, tests or intervals. Its 29-version summary conflicts with 28 modifications implied by the design, and its improvement counts combine different constructs. The positive structural observations remain evidence; they do not establish guaranteed preservation, equivalent effort or all-assignment benefit.

## S85 complete: explicit migration policy, solver costs and later live-modeling alternatives

[S85](full-reading/S85-constraint-state-migration.md) is fully read: thirteen publisher pages, five tables, two figures, three listings and 29 references. Eight page images, including the acquisition first page, cover all visual/code displays. The already attached user PDF is byte-identical to CWI's copy, so no duplicate was uploaded. Nextep separates new-state invariants from designer-specified migration rules and a distance heuristic. Its robot example changes pending queues after a new dependency; it is not a physical trial or proof of arbitrary external-effect rollback. Seamless return to the interpreter/user remains future work. The conceptual RMPatch comparison does not measure developer benefit.

The synthetic state-machine benchmark has frequent solver timeouts under a three-second solving limit, plus translation costs. The paper prints 9/19 and 13/19 timed-out configurations; its figure and released six-to-25 interval cover twenty sizes. A dated GitHub check at `b3024f49818eefc0fab18bb6dddaadb96c331453` (2 November 2018) downloaded and hashed fourteen selected files, without running the tool or claiming experiment-build equivalence. Stored raw/aggregate arithmetic recovers the figure's timeout lists; the wider two sets each have 35 sizes/350 observations. All 304 checked “mean” cells, including the separate sixty-observation `scenario1b`, match a source function averaging the sixth/seventh sorted values for ten runs, not an arithmetic mean or conventional median. Publication plot generation, phase clocks and exact build remain unverified. Printed robotic navigation expressions differ from the source; queue-order conventions need verification. The full note preserves these bounds instead of treating source availability as reproduction.

W21 opened the cited mutable benchmark directory and the AlleAlle repository. The former returned a web cache error; direct GitHub API access subsequently found the dated commit/tree and selected raw files. Current AlleAlle README coverage does not establish the 2018 dependency version. No VM, solver or generated candidate was executed, and all artifacts remain outside Git.

SC25 requested RMPatch (`10.1007/s10270-017-0608-7`), Echo (`10.1109/ASE.2013.6693135`) and the Kodkod assessment (`10.1007/978-3-642-21470-7_6`), together with the exact UML/OCL synchronization title, using `limit:20`, offset 0. **Three records** returned, matching the DOI targets. Metadata, supplied short/missing abstracts and indexed passages were inspected. SC26 queried *On Leveraging UML/OCL for Model Synchronization* alone with the same page size/offset and returned **zero**. Its [ten-page CEUR file](https://ceur-ws.org/Vol-1706/paper4.pdf) is reachable at file-metadata coverage; the body remains unread. These exact requests account for locators, not the complete transformation/policy literature.

An incoming Scite graph for S85, depth 1 and `max_edges:20`, returned **eight edges/nine nodes**, untruncated, with no low-coverage seed flag. This is still only resolved index coverage, not a complete citing field. `s` cites `t`; all eight edges point to S85. SC27 requested the seven new identities with `limit:20`, offset 0 and returned **seven** records: the generated-code live-modeling journal `10.1007/s10270-020-00829-y`, Live-UMLRT tool paper `10.1109/MODELS-C.2019.00115`, finite-model-finder live programming `10.1109/ASE56229.2023.00016`, live model checking `10.1145/3732771.3742718`, Bacatá `10.22152/programming-journal.org/2020/4/11`, sustainability evaluation `10.1145/3371906`, and the proceedings-level `10.1145/3623476`. The eighth citer is the already read S73. Supplied abstracts/snippets and title identities were inspected; a proceedings record is not an independent experiment, and neither a citation label nor an eight-edge neighborhood establishes validation.

W22 used exact titles for finite model finders, generated-code live modeling/PDF, and integrated model checking; seventeen web identities were screened, plus the direct CEUR file. The [Sullivan author abstract](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.17317) proposes closer interaction between written constraints and solutions; it is not evidence that Nextep's solve-time problem has been eliminated. Author-PDF opening/index coverage, duplicate conference/index locators and an institutional publication list remain distinct from a full reading. [The SLE 2025 conference abstract](https://conf.researchr.org/details/sle-2025/sle-2025-papers/16/Integrating-Model-Checking-into-a-Live-Modeling-Environment) introduces model-checking witness traces relative to current execution, integrated with edit/execution history. The [B01–B12 map](nu-background-survey-2026-09-30.md) now separates discovery coverage, primary understanding, evidence disposition and next action. It preserves positive capabilities/results along with null/adverse findings and explicitly distinguishes empirical uncertainty from unread or inaccessible methods. It does not declare any theme complete. PLAN/AGENTS are no longer edited to duplicate counts. D1's both-exhaustive baselines, diagnostic-site/behavior distinction, finite-observation contract and legitimate adverse outcomes are accepted without launching its feasibility work.

This is **targeted extracted journal coverage, not a complete or visually verified journal read**; no local journal hash/PDF attachment exists. The original preprint remains the counted full edition. Local arithmetic checks sums, descriptive means and inferred score consistency; no statistical test, author code, tool installation, recovery run or experiment occurred. The repository README is read, but anonymous GitHub API rate limiting returned 403 before a pinned implementation could be obtained.

**Mechanism and result:** SyDRA combines manually assigned sixteen-category subsystems with include graphs, then supplies models/navigation alongside VS Code. Ten-engine preparation took about six months, including two months of first-author manual classification. The human comparison has eight independent people per arm, one engine and nine understanding/impact-location tasks, with no implementation. Descriptive understanding/precision directions favor support, whereas impact time is greater. Its proposed O3DE reorganization is unimplemented.

**Unresolved reconstruction:** printed group means do not average to printed pooled means; raw understanding means are 72.963/59.086 minutes and impact means 21.379/41.368, differing from Table 6. Four completeness scores exceed the declared maximum two; inferred denominators are eleven/three, with some correct counts exceeding them. Two empty-answer cases suppress the other task’s nonzero precision. Paired inference has no justified pairing for independent groups, and the released p-value column contains values above one. These are not silently repaired, no corrected significance is claimed, and positive descriptive directions remain recorded. The paper’s 57% no-engine-experience statement also differs from released seven/eight-of-sixteen counts under two questions.

Sixteen experienced users, seven professional/nine advanced students, undertake four ordered tasks with balanced randomized condition histories. Own arithmetic verifies all sixteen histories and the task means. The first three source comparisons have roughly **78%, 83% and 41% lower measured modular times**; the fourth has almost equal totals. The endpoint excludes typing and halves second-sitting logic time in four monolithic attempts. Restoring the omitted half strengthens the positive differences but cannot recover unclocked refamiliarization or total labor. **Evidence disposition:** the four reported adjusted R² values are .15/.36/.42/.58. Positive effort–churn correlations .59 for modified files/.66 for new files, and lower effort for smaller system C, remain useful findings. Revisions are measured during the work and used as a quality proxy after few acceptance defects were observed. This conditional model does not identify a total smell/refactoring or size-reduction effect.
